 \delta r=d_c\cos\phi+d_s\sin\phi,
 \qquad d_c=L_5\delta\Psi,\quad d_s=L_5(i\delta\Psi).
\]
It is not generally legitimate to replace this by $e^{i\phi}d_c$.
A fixed-amplitude phase sweep traces an ellipse in the two-column
response plane, not every vector in that plane. Free quadrature
controls or variable amplitude are extra assumptions; any control
or energy cap further restricts attainability.

\subsection{A necessary reachability test before optimizing phases}

Let $E$ be the real finite residual space with its fixed calibrated
inner product, and let $J:\mathbb R^k\to E$ be the evaluated local
response of the allowed controls. These may include fit parameters
and relay quadratures. If differentiable guard measurements give
$Gu=0$ at first order, choose a basis $Z$ of $\ker G$ and put
$J_g=JZ$. Discrete admissibility flags cannot be differentiated
instead of being recomputed. Let $P_g$ be the orthogonal projector
onto $\operatorname{Ran}J_g$ in the declared residual norm.

\begin{proposition}[Best unconstrained linearized C5 lowering]
For a nonzero residual $r$, unrestricted tangent controls give
\[
 \inf_\eta\|r+J_g\eta\|^2=\|(I-P_g)r\|^2.
\]
Writing $p=\|P_gr\|^2/\|r\|^2$, a squared-score ratio at most
$3/4$ is attainable in this linear model if and only if $p\ge1/4$.
With relaxation $\alpha\in[0,1]$ of the least-squares cancellation,
\[
 r^+=(I-\alpha P_g)r,\qquad
 \frac{\|r^+\|^2}{\|r\|^2}=1-(2\alpha-\alpha^2)p.
\]
For $p\ge1/4$, the choice
$\alpha=1-\sqrt{1-1/(4p)}$ gives exactly $3/4$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Decompose $r=P_gr+(I-P_g)r$. An allowed control can cancel the
first part, while no allowed control changes the orthogonal part.
Orthogonality gives both the infimum and the relaxation identity.
The minimum ratio is $1-p$, proving the threshold and formula.
\end{proof}

This is an optimistic necessary test for a bounded physical control:
finite amplitude, phase-circle restrictions, energy budgets and
nonlinear guards can only increase its minimum. If $J_g$ has rank
below $\dim E$, some residual direction has $p=0$; no uniform
strict contraction of the entire residual space follows from these
controls alone. That direction need not occur in a smaller native
witness family, which must be specified before applying this
obstruction. A contracting baseline can also change the test: apply
it to the actual baseline residual rather than silently using the
identity update.

First-order guard tangency is not exact guard invariance, and a
tight linearized $3/4$ estimate leaves no margin for nonlinear
errors. A uniform slack estimate or an exact nonlinear construction
is needed for a genuine finite-step certificate.

\subsection{An explicit three-sample rank and nonlinear return test}

Take a deliberately declared minimal sample family from the earlier
ellipse diagnostic, with unit weights, fixed axis $e=(1,0)$ and
one sample in each labelled residual block:
\[
 x_1=(2,0),\qquad x_2=(0,\sqrt3),\qquad
 x_3(\vartheta)=\operatorname{Rot}(\vartheta)(1,3/2).
\]
The reference is $(A,c,\vartheta)=(2,1,0)$, where all three focal
sums equal $4$. The third control is a \emph{physical displacement
of one sample relative to the fixed foci}, not a passive frame
rephasing. Its realization by the native relay has not been proved.
Direct differentiation gives the exact Jacobians
\[
 J_{A,c}=\begin{pmatrix}-2&0\\-2&1\\-2&4/5\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad n=\frac{(-1,-4,5)^T}{\sqrt{42}},
 \qquad J_{A,c}^Tn=0,
\]
\[
 J_{A,c,\vartheta}=
 \begin{pmatrix}-2&0&0\\-2&1&0\\-2&4/5&2/5\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad \det J_{A,c,\vartheta}=-4/5\ne0.
\]
For example $b_3=(4/5,8/5)$ and
$\partial_\vartheta x_3=(-3/2,1)$ at the reference, so their
scalar product is $2/5$. The two fit parameters leave the
$n$-direction inaccessible to first order; the specified sample
response removes that rank defect. Three labels alone did not do so.

The inverse-function theorem now gives a local inverse for this
\emph{constructed} residual map $R_{\rm lens}(A,c,\vartheta)$.
Choose a sufficiently small residual ball
inside its image. The exact local update
\[
 F=R_{\rm lens}^{-1}\circ
       \left(\frac{\sqrt3}{2}I\right)\circ R_{\rm lens}
\]
is defined on its preimage and obeys
\[
 R_{\rm lens}(Fp)=\frac{\sqrt3}{2}R_{\rm lens}(p),\qquad
 \Phi_{\rm lens}(Fp)=\frac34\Phi_{\rm lens}(p).
\]
The residual ball is invariant under this scaling, so the local
model supports all finite iterates there. This is a nonlinear
coordinate realization, not a proof that the physical relay
implements the inverse-map update.

A narrowly specified geometric guard can be preserved exactly:
take $K=\{0\}$ and $\varepsilon=3$ in
$g_i=d(x_i,K)-\varepsilon$. Changing $A,c$ does not move the
samples, and rotating $x_3$ preserves its radius. All three $g_i$
are therefore constant with positive margins
$\varepsilon-\|x_i\|$. This is a declared diagnostic anchor,
not the native HC anchor or the full r04 guard tuple. If the real
guard disallows this angular motion, the third column must be
removed; the rank defect then returns. A larger sample family also
need not be controllable by these three parameters.

\paragraph{Executed nonlinear and rejection checks.}
The companion script \texttt{OP5\_Relay\_C5\_Check.py} implements
the real, norm-whitened response test with an SVD and optional
linear guard constraints. It rejects zero-score ratios and
nonfinite input. Its diagnostics assume unbounded tangent controls
and explicitly report that neither native readout identity nor
nonlinear guard invariance has been certified. Numerical rank
decisions are floating-point tests, not rigorous spectral-gap proofs.

The constructed nonlinear fit was checked at 80 decimal digits.
An initial target $R_0=10^{-3}n$ was inverted near $(2,1,0)$,
then twelve updates used $R_{k+1}=(\sqrt3/2)R_k$. The initial
score was $10^{-6}$ and the final score was
\[
 10^{-6}(3/4)^{12}=3.16763520240783691\times10^{-8}.
\]
The largest discrepancy in an individual squared-score ratio was
below $9.1\times10^{-62}$, and the largest squared-radius discrepancy
below $8.5\times10^{-81}$. All iterates stayed inside the declared
test box $1.9<A<2.1$, $0.9<c<1.1$, $|\vartheta|<0.1$.
The factor $3/4$ was selected as the target: these checks establish
attainability in this construction, not a predicted physical decay rate.
They are not an interval-arithmetic certificate or the source's
original finite scan. A separate exact symbolic differentiation also
verified the displayed Jacobian, null direction, determinant and
radial-guard identity directly from the lens formula.
Seven reproducible tests passed, including an unreachable direction,
the exact quarter-accessibility threshold, a full-rank augmentation
and rejection of that augmentation when a linear guard fixes its
third control. No genuine native coupling has been fitted to make
the answer equal $3/4$.

\paragraph{What this establishes and what to evaluate next.}
There is now an executed response-to-score test, a source-linked
geometric derivative and an exact local construction showing how
an additional allowed channel can remove a missing residual
direction. The concrete native task is to evaluate the complete
retarded field response above, map it through the actual trajectory
and witness extraction, and compare its residual column with the
existing fit columns \emph{after} imposing the real guards.
That test may fail through rank, amplitude budget or guard coupling
even if transport exists. The missing native $R_5$, its dictionary
to the lens fit and the evaluated vertices remain explicit data
obligations; the construction is not a gravitational or global
OP5 solution and does not explain the separate Riemann benchmark
without its own readout identification.

\section{Executed frame-value transport with an explicit HC guard}
\label{sec:frame-value-hc-transport}

\paragraph{Inputs and hypotheses, not a recovered native dictionary.}
The source's symmetric frame gives $\beta=0.5493$ approximately;
its cascade reading supplies the probability $w=\beta^2$, and its
Dirac-probe clock displays $e^{i\pi\beta}$. A common scalar clock
phase preserves every quadratic norm. Holding the GUE operator and
projector fixed also gives
\[
 [e^{i\pi\beta}R_{\rm GUE},Q]
   =e^{i\pi\beta}[R_{\rm GUE},Q],
\]
so its absolute commutator mismatch cannot change from that scalar
factor alone. An evaluated change of clock spectrum, projector,
coupling or physical readout would be needed for a different result.

The present calculation therefore tests an explicit extra realization:
two intermediate spectral modes have phases $e^{\pm i\phi}$,
$\phi=\pi\beta$, and the visible fit has their weights $(w,1-w)$.
The negative phase is a mirrored extension of the displayed clock,
and a cascade probability is not automatically a C5 spectral weight.
These are declared hypotheses. Unlike the earlier inverse-fit test,
the visible score below is computed from these inputs, not assigned
the target $3/4$. The native $R_5$ and its embedding remain unevaluated.

\subsection{Why the direct raw-frame assignment fails the fixed HC test}

On the diagnostic three-mode carrier let
\[
 J_s=\begin{pmatrix}0&1&0\\1&0&0\\0&0&1\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad C_s=(I+J_s)/2,
 \qquad f_+=(1,1,0)^T/\sqrt2.
\]
Assigning the opposite phases directly to the raw exchanged pair
would give $D=\operatorname{diag}(e^{i\phi},e^{-i\phi},1)$.
Exact multiplication yields
\[
 \|(I-C_s)Df_+\|^2=\sin^2\phi,
 \qquad \|[D,J_s]\|_{\rm F}^2=8\sin^2\phi.
\]
With the stated frame value these are respectively
$0.9762032204$ and $7.8096257633$. Thus this $D$ cannot serve as
a fixed-$J_s$ admissible MM transport preserving the HC-even input
sector. This conditional obstruction concerns that direct raw-frame
identification, not every possible native frame readout. If the
projector is passively co-transported to $DC_sD^\dagger$ the image
constraint changes consistently, but co-transporting the fit
selector as well leaves the measured norm unchanged. That is not
a physical lowering mechanism.

\subsection{A guard-compatible realization within the even sector}

The even sector is two-dimensional: the pair combination $f_+$
and the independent orthogonal probe mode $e_\perp$ are both
$J_s$-even. The spectral phases can act \emph{within} this sector
without being assigned to the raw matter/tachyon slots. In the
$(f_+,e_\perp)$ basis define
\[
 S_w=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt w&\sqrt{1-w}\\
                     \sqrt{1-w}&-\sqrt w\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 U_+=S_w\begin{pmatrix}e^{i\phi}&0\\0&e^{-i\phi}\end{pmatrix}S_w^T.
\]
Extend $U_+$ by the identity on the odd combination $f_-$ to
obtain $U$ on the three-mode carrier. Put
\[
 a=\cos\phi+i(2w-1)\sin\phi,
 \qquad b=2i\sqrt{w(1-w)}\sin\phi.
\]
Then
\[
 Uf_+=a f_++b e_\perp,\qquad
 U^\dagger U=I,\qquad [U,J_s]=[U,C_s]=0,
 \qquad C_sUf_+=Uf_+.
\]
For the fixed visible selector $P=|f_+\rangle\langle f_+|$,
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 q=|a|^2=1-4w(1-w)\sin^2\phi
      =0.17729809232764920886,\qquad |b|^2=1-q.}
\]
This is a strong visible lowering with full norm conserved and
zero HC-odd leakage in this realization. The unseen fraction is
inside HC, not outside the licensed sector. The source's $\perp$
mode remains a probe degree of freedom, not an identified Maxwell
photon or wormhole throat.

For an abstract residual Hilbert space $E$, the construction can
be tensored with $I_E$. Its visible compression is $aI_E$, so it
has the uniform one-step squared-norm bound $q<3/4$ on that
declared space. Real residuals require an explicitly compatible
realification and quadrature norm. Identification with the actual
three C5 blocks still requires the original residual metric and
witness dictionary; tensoring does not provide them.
Any licensed gate $Q$ containing the visible and history images
and commuting with $U$ preserves those images. The actual $C_7$
gate has not been extracted. Protected C4/C6 registers can be
kept separate in the model, but no native dependency map has
established their invariance under this transport.

\paragraph{The generator conditions are now explicit.}
One time-generator representative realizing this step in duration
$\tau>0$, in units $\hbar=1$, is
\[
 H_+=-\frac{\phi}{\tau}
 \begin{pmatrix}
 2w-1&2\sqrt{w(1-w)}\\
 2\sqrt{w(1-w)}&1-2w
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad U_+=e^{-i\tau H_+}.
\]
Thus the off-diagonal rate magnitude must obey
$\kappa\tau=2\phi\sqrt{w(1-w)}$ and the diagonal detuning
magnitude obeys $|\Delta|\tau=2\phi|2w-1|$ for this representative.
The phase and weight do not fix $\tau$ or a unique generator;
logarithm branches and overall phases also matter. To match the
native even triad, its kinetic reduction must produce these rates
from $\chi\mathcal T_{1\perp}$ and the diagonal resonances,
not by setting them after asking for a desired score.
No such evaluated equality is present in the inspected source.

\subsection{The second step disproves repeated lowering by this fixed channel}

Keeping the same finite carrier and applying $U$ again gives
\[
 q_n=\|PU^nf_+\|^2
     =1-4w(1-w)\sin^2(n\phi).
\]
For the supplied frame value,
\[
 (q_1,q_2,q_3,q_4)
 =(0.1772980923,\ 0.9216893762,\ 0.3264651774,\ 0.7158646149).
\]
In particular $q_2/q_1\approx5.19853$, not a number at most
$3/4$. Previously stored amplitude returns from $e_\perp$.
The homogeneous history term cannot be discarded merely because
the visible first-step score was small. The finite unitary model
therefore proves a conditional single-step bound and \emph{fails}
the repeated C5-lowering requirement.

The prepared history construction shows exactly what would repair
this at the model level. On $E\oplus\ell^2(\mathbb N_0;E)$ define
\[
 V(\xi,h_0,h_1,\ldots)=(a\xi,b\xi,h_0,h_1,\ldots).
\]
Since $|a|^2+|b|^2=1$, this is norm-preserving. Starting with
empty history, its visible component is $a^n\xi_0$ and its score
is $q^n\|\xi_0\|^2$. The old history is shifted, not reset or
destroyed. $V$ is an isometry, not a surjective unitary on this
one-sided space; a closed unitary realization requires its own
incoming/history dilation. Unlimited history is not supplied by
a finite native matrix or finite positive budget automatically.
All-history gate compatibility, native admissibility and physical
realization remain obligations. The protected C4 register cannot
be used as that history reservoir.

\paragraph{Executed checks and exact outcome.}
Wolfram independently verified the symbolic weighted compression,
unitarity, fixed-duality commutation, HC-even image, total norm and
the contrasting raw-frame leakage. The companion script reproduces
the numerical frame test and explicitly tests the second-step
failure. Its eleven tests pass, including the earlier lens and
guard-rejection tests. Numerical and symbolic checks are distinct
from extracting a physical coupling or native witness.

The first-step guard issue is now narrowed: place the fit/history
mixing inside an admissible HC sector, rather than treating opposite
raw-frame phases as an admissible MM converter. Repeated lowering
additionally needs justified one-way history transport, damping or
another evaluated return-prevention mechanism. The source's
unevaluated C5 extraction and C4/C6 guard dependencies prevent
promotion of this calculation to a native OP5 proof. It is not
evidence for a physical wormhole or superluminal channel.

\section{A proved no-return realization and its native spectral test}
\label{sec:proved-no-return-realization}

\paragraph{The strengthened question.}
The last frame-value test left a precise obstruction: a reused
finite two-mode unitary returns history to the visible fit. This
section proves a finite autonomous no-return implementation, a
local causal amplitude-port realization, and quantitative return
limits. These establish realizability under declared embeddings
and boundary conditions, not identity with the native C5 witness.
The distinction between the source's QAM tag shift, an amplitude
history shift and actual spatial propagation remains essential.

\subsection{An autonomous unitary conveyor for a finite horizon}

Keep the previous frame-derived amplitudes $a,b$, with
$|a|^2=q$, $|b|^2=1-q$, $b$ purely imaginary, and
$q\approx0.1772980923$. Its local scattering matrix is
\[
 S=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\b&a^*\end{pmatrix},\qquad S^\dagger S=I.
\]
For a positive integer $N$, take a fixed carrier
$\mathcal K_N=E_{\rm vis}\oplus E_0\oplus\cdots\oplus E_{N-1}$.
Apply $S$ to the visible amplitude and slot $0$, leaving the other
slots fixed. Then cyclically advance the history slots so that the
newly emitted slot becomes the last slot. The resulting autonomous
unitary is
\[
 U_N(\xi,h_0,h_1,\ldots,h_{N-1})
 =\bigl(a\xi+bh_0,\ h_1,\ldots,h_{N-1},\ b\xi+a^*h_0\bigr).
\]
It is a unitary scattering followed by a unitary permutation.
Starting with empty history, direct induction gives for $0\le n\le N$
\[
 \xi_n=a^n\xi_0,\qquad
 (h_0,\ldots,h_{N-1})_n
 =(0,\ldots,0,b\xi_0,ba\xi_0,\ldots,ba^{n-1}\xi_0).
\]
The next active input remains empty through those $N$ applications.
Hence the visible score is $q^n\|\xi_0\|^2$, the history norm is
$(1-q^n)\|\xi_0\|^2$, and no amplitude has been erased.
On application $N+1$, the oldest record returns:
\[
 \xi_{N+1}=(a^{N+1}+b^2)\xi_0.
\]
This makes the finite validity horizon explicit rather than
silently treating a closed finite memory as an unlimited reservoir.

The script checks $N=20$ on a fixed unitary carrier and obtains
\[
 \frac{\Phi_{20}}{\Phi_0}
 =q^{20}\approx9.4206583741\times10^{-16},\qquad
 \frac{\Phi_{21}}{\Phi_0}\approx0.6768384501.
\]
The large return is not a numerical instability. The maximum
relative discrepancy from $q^n$ through step 20 was below
$2.1\times10^{-15}$; the fit-plus-history norm discrepancy was
below $2.3\times10^{-16}$. Separate protected and forbidden test
registers were respectively fixed and unexcited. These are declared
model projections, not evaluations of the native r04 guard functions.
This frame-model recurrence is also not the source's exact
$(3/4)^n$ ledger recurrence, although it satisfies the stronger
single-step inequality on its no-return interval.

\paragraph{A sharp capacity bound for a fixed power dilation.}
Suppose a fixed unitary $U$ and fit isometry $\iota:E\to\mathcal K$
obey $\iota^\dagger U^n\iota=a^nI_E$ for $0\le n\le N$,
with $|a|<1$ and finite dimension $d=\dim E$. Then
\[
 \dim\mathcal K\ge(N+1)d.
\]
Indeed the Gram operator of the maps
$\iota,U\iota,\ldots,U^N\iota$ is $T_N\otimes I_E$, where
\[
 (T_N)_{jk}=
 \begin{cases}a^{k-j},&k\ge j,\\(a^*)^{j-k},&j>k.\end{cases}
\]
It is positive definite: a lower triangular Cholesky factor has
$L_{j0}=(a^*)^j$ and
$L_{jk}=(a^*)^{j-k}\sqrt{1-|a|^2}$ for $1\le k\le j$.
Thus $\det T_N=(1-|a|^2)^N>0$, and the $(N+1)d$ images are
linearly independent. The conveyor attains this bound before
independent protected coordinates are counted.

This is a bound for the stated \emph{fixed, uniform amplitude}
power-dilation requirement, not every scalar score sequence.
Dimensions must be counted over the same field and metric: three
complex fit directions require 63 complex coordinates at $N=20$;
they are not three real scalar slots. A matrix labelled
$\mathcal M_{16}$ may be an internal operator on a larger spatial
carrier, so this count is not a general impossibility result for
the Companion. Native licensed embeddings still need evaluation.

Time-dependent controllers can avoid this fixed-carrier bound.
For example the unitary matrices
\[
 B_n=\begin{pmatrix}a^n&-\sqrt{1-q^n}\\
                    \sqrt{1-q^n}&(a^*)^n\end{pmatrix}
\]
give stage-dependent steps $B_nB_{n-1}^\dagger$ and the prepared
orbit $(a^n,\sqrt{1-q^n})^T$ with only two amplitude coordinates.
This compresses a known coherent history through an explicitly
stage-dependent control; it does not provide a fixed autonomous
transport or store arbitrary independent incoming histories.
The required native time-dependent coupling is a separate obligation.

\subsection{A local causal port whose output cannot return}

There is also a continuum realization in which the outgoing
record is spatial rather than a growing symbolic list. Let
$\xi(t)\in E$ be the visible amplitude, $u(t)$ its incoming
port amplitude, and $y(t)$ its outgoing amplitude. For a declared
rate $\lambda>0$ and real $\omega$, set
\[
 \dot\xi=-(\lambda/2+i\omega)\xi-\sqrt\lambda\,u,
 \qquad y=u+\sqrt\lambda\,\xi.
\]
This is a model boundary law, not an extracted Sphaera equation.
Differentiation proves its local balance exactly:
\[
 \frac{d}{dt}\|\xi\|^2+\|y\|^2-\|u\|^2=0.
\]
In particular the reduced damping is transfer to output, not loss
of the combined norm. On a finite window $[0,T]$ the linear map
\[
 (\xi(0),u|_{[0,T]})\longmapsto(\xi(T),y|_{[0,T]})
\]
preserves the norm on $E\oplus L^2([0,T];E)$. It is onto:
given $\xi(T)$ and $y$, solve backward
$\dot\xi=(\lambda/2-i\omega)\xi-\sqrt\lambda y$ and recover
$u=y-\sqrt\lambda\xi$. Bounded finite-window ODE solution operators
provide the inverse. Thus the joint input/output map is unitary,
even though the visible subsystem alone is damped.

Embed the field in a one-way guide with velocity $0<v\le c$:
\[
 (\partial_t+v\partial_x)z=0\quad(x\ne0),\qquad
 u=\sqrt v\,z(0^-,t),\quad y=\sqrt v\,z(0^+,t).
\]
The guide has local norm flux
$\partial_t\|z\|^2+v\partial_x\|z\|^2=0$ away from the port.
With empty incoming excitations and no initial guide field, the
explicit output is
\[
 z(x,t)=\sqrt{\lambda/v}\,
 e^{-(\lambda/2+i\omega)(t-x/v)}\xi_0
 \quad(0<x<vt),
\]
and is zero outside its outgoing causal support. Consequently
\[
 \|\xi(t)\|^2=e^{-\lambda t}\|\xi_0\|^2,
 \qquad \int_0^{vt}\!\|z(x,t)\|^2dx
   =(1-e^{-\lambda t})\|\xi_0\|^2.
\]
The emitted record moves away; the right-moving equation has no
left-moving reflection channel. This proves causal no-return for
this boundary/guide model. A finite reflecting guide, dispersion
or a different native operator can destroy that property.
Temporal retardation by itself does not forbid later return.

For a declared sampling interval $\tau$, the previous complex
frame amplitude is reproduced by
\[
 \lambda\tau=-\log q\approx1.7299228255,\qquad
 \omega\tau=-\arg a\approx1.9458894365
 \quad\text{modulo }2\pi.
\]
These are matching conditions, not rates predicted by the
frame offset alone. The script checks finite-window conservation
and its inverse at 80 decimal digits using a nonzero complex
incoming signal; both errors were below $5.3\times10^{-82}$.
The outgoing pulse, transport equation and integrated history norm
were also verified by symbolic differentiation and integration.

This construction concerns amplitudes and their declared positive
norm, not a derived four-dimensional stress tensor or a Maxwell
photon. Zero incoming excitation amplitude is not the zero quantum
vacuum state or the disappearance of quantum input fluctuations.
Spectral stability, finite bandwidth and the microscopic origin
of the boundary law have not been established. In particular a
global state of finite Hamiltonian variance initially wholly in
the visible space has zero first derivative of its survival
probability; an exact exponential from $t=0$ is not automatically
that regular closed-system survival law. A coarse-grained port
description needs its own regime and error estimates.

The related established framework is Gardiner and Collett,
\href{https://journals.aps.org/pra/abstract/10.1103/PhysRevA.31.3761}{
\emph{Input and output in damped quantum systems}}, which keeps
bath inputs, outputs, causality and reversed dynamics explicit.
That reference motivates the comparison; the balance and finite-window
inverse above are proved here for the displayed amplitude equations.

\subsection{Which native spectrum would have to produce this port}

The native $\Psi_\perp(x)$ is a spacetime field, not necessarily
one finite oscillator. Its spatial modes could supply a larger
carrier, but an outgoing spectrum and its coupling weights must
actually be calculated. After a justified positive time-generator
reduction, let $H_X$ be self-adjoint and $V$ the licensed coupling.
If its spectral measure has an absolutely continuous density on
the relevant window, define the operator-valued density
\[
 \mathcal J_X(\nu)
 =V\frac{dE_{H_X}(\nu)}{d\nu}V^\dagger\ge0,
 \qquad
 \Sigma^{\rm ret}(\Omega)
 =\int\frac{\mathcal J_X(\nu)}{\Omega-\nu+i0}\,d\nu.
\]
For a continuous density, the imaginary part follows from
$\operatorname{Im}(x+i\epsilon)^{-1}
=-\epsilon/(x^2+\epsilon^2)$:
\[
 -2\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^{\rm ret}(\Omega)
 =2\pi\mathcal J_X(\Omega).
\]
Thus the scalar port requires a density giving approximately
$2\pi\mathcal J_X(\Omega)=\lambda I_E$ across its operating
window, with the real principal-value shift included in $\omega$.
The return kernel must be short or otherwise bounded relative to
the actual update interval; a Markov replacement is not licensed
by an operator name. A finite isolated spectrum gives poles rather
than this smooth irreversible response. A spectral gap at the
operating frequency gives no such continuum damping there.

This narrows the native task to measurable operator data:
the spectral density of the constrained intermediate field,
its matrix elements through $\chi\mathcal T_{a\perp}$,
the allowed outgoing boundary condition, and their map through
the actual C5 witness extraction. Neither $w=\beta^2$ nor the
frame phase evaluates $\mathcal J_X$.

\subsection{Quantitative tolerance for an imperfect outgoing channel}

For a discrete incoming amplitude $\eta_n$, the exact local
scatterer gives $\xi_{n+1}=a\xi_n+b\eta_n$. If
$\|\eta_n\|\le\epsilon\|\xi_n\|$, then
\[
 \|\xi_{n+1}\|\le
 (\sqrt q+\sqrt{1-q}\,\epsilon)\|\xi_n\|.
\]
A sufficient worst-phase condition for squared-score lowering
by at most $3/4$ is
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \epsilon\le\frac{\sqrt{3/4}-\sqrt q}{\sqrt{1-q}}
 \approx0.4905659271.}
\]
For the exact original $q=3/4$ model this worst-phase tolerance
is zero. The stronger frame-model one-step bound has slack, but
the tolerance is relative to the shrinking visible amplitude,
not the initial charge.

For example, consider the first isolated return of the oldest
emission after $N$ steps, with amplitude reflection coefficient
$r_{\rm refl}$. Before any earlier return,
$\eta_N=r_{\rm refl}b\xi_0$ and $\xi_N=a^N\xi_0$. The
above sufficient condition becomes
\[
 |r_{\rm refl}|\le
 \frac{\sqrt{3/4}-\sqrt q}{1-q}\,q^{N/2}.
\]
At $N=20$ it is approximately $1.66003\times10^{-8}$.
This is a single-return worst-phase amplitude bound; coherent
multiple returns need their full summed-kernel estimate. It shows
why a small absolute reflection can be large compared with an
already tiny visible residual. For a fixed finite horizon, a
propagation delay excluding return during that horizon is another
solution; it does not prove permanent no-return.

\subsection{Guard preservation and source-compatible registration}

Let the permitted amplitude copies reduce an orthogonal licensed
gate $Q$, let $J$ act evenly on them, and let scatterer and history
transport intertwine these same selectors. Then their product has
zero off-$Q$ debt and zero HC-odd leakage. Extending it by the
identity on a specified protected C4 space gives
$P_4U=UP_4=P_4$, so the protected vector and its norm are unchanged.
These are exact operator statements about the declared decomposition.
They become statements about r04 only after those projections are
identified with the actual leakage/absorption/scope evaluations.
Keeping a copied zero flag is not that identification.

The active QAM/MML/MMA store-array layer supplies finite store states,
fresh stored objects, links, lineage and explicit scrub operations
through \texttt{def:qam-store-state},
\texttt{def:mml-store-instructions} and
\texttt{def:mma-store-transition}. A finite outgoing record can be
registered at fresh local addresses with its parent, emitted amplitude,
selector and step. This uses the existing namespace rather than
performing a global QAM tag migration. The actual guards and links
must pass the store/array scrub before a transition is called
admissible; the source explicitly forbids manufacturing certificates
by command syntax. Coding a history is not proof of a physical
guide, nor of native closure-class equality.

Finally the native score must select the visible component rather
than counting all stored history. If it counts the full preserved
norm, its ratio is one and none of these mechanisms lowers C5.
The unprovided residual dictionary, native guard dependencies and
spectral density therefore remain indispensable.

\paragraph{Verification and status.}
Fifteen executable tests now pass. They cover the finite twenty-step
conveyor, explicit first recirculation, protected/forbidden test
registers, the continuum-port balance and inverse, and the earlier
fit/frame diagnostics. Exact symbolic checks separately verified
the flux identity, outgoing advection, history integral and an
instance of the power-dilation Gram determinant; the general
capacity argument is given above.

The no-return mechanism is now proved for an explicit causal
norm-conserving port and for a finite autonomous unitary horizon.
It is not proved to be the source's physical intermediate field.
The next native obligation is no longer an unspecified ``fresh
history'' assumption: compute a licensed outgoing spectral density
and return bound, then evaluate the actual C5 extraction and guards.
No gravitational, wormhole, all-readout or exact finite-zero claim
is promoted by this construction.

\section{Native outgoing audit: shared-channel rank and slow modes}
\label{sec:native-outgoing-rank-audit}

\paragraph{Source boundary and scope.}
The active source's \texttt{prop:triadic-two-probe} supplies
$\Psi_\perp$, $\Omega_\perp$ and the vertices
$\chi\mathcal T_{a\perp}$, but not their evaluated dispersion,
polarizations or outgoing boundary condition. The core/Hawking
block \texttt{thm:core} describes boundary discharge structurally;
it does not evaluate a retarded Green function, a reflection
coefficient or the C5 extraction. Its designation as a theorem
does not supply those missing calculations. In particular, a
photon-frame label does not by itself select a one-way massless
propagation operator.

Assume, only for this audit, that a positive-current Hamiltonian
reduction and reciprocal vertices have been licensed. For two
scalar probe amplitudes and one open bath polarization at frequency
$\nu$, let $v(\nu)=(v_1(\nu),v_2(\nu))^T$ be the evaluated
coupling vector and $\rho(\nu)\geq0$ its spectral density. Then
\[
 \mathcal J_X(\nu)=\rho(\nu)v(\nu)v(\nu)^\dagger,
 \qquad \Gamma(\nu)=2\pi\mathcal J_X(\nu),
 \qquad \operatorname{rank}\Gamma(\nu)\leq1.
\]
This is an exact rank calculation, not an assertion that the native
field has only one polarization. Orthogonality of the third
component does not determine its spectral multiplicity. Multiple
open polarizations contribute a sum of such positive matrices.

\subsection{An exact dark combination, with an important qualification}

For equal vertices $v_1=v_2$, set
\[
 b=(\Psi_1+\Psi_2)/\sqrt2,\qquad
 d=(\Psi_1-\Psi_2)/\sqrt2.
\]
The channel couples to $b$ and annihilates $d$. If, in addition,
the probe Hamiltonian preserves these combinations and the kernel
has this same coupling direction throughout the relevant frequency
band, $d$ is an invariant dark mode. A single-frequency rank defect
alone is insufficient: Hamiltonian mixing or rotating coupling
directions can eventually expose an instantaneous dark vector to
the bath.

In the constant Markov reduction with bright score-decay rate
$\lambda>0$, no incoming field and no bright--dark mixing,
\[
 \|r(t)\|^2=e^{-\lambda t}|b_0|^2+|d_0|^2.
\]
Thus uniform contraction on the whole two-probe space is impossible,
even with perfect outward propagation. On a particular state, with
$p=|b_0|^2/\|r_0\|^2$ and $q=e^{-\lambda\tau}$,
\[
 \frac{\|r(\tau)\|^2}{\|r_0\|^2}=1-(1-q)p\leq\frac34
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 p\geq\frac{1}{4(1-q)}.
\]
For the earlier conditional frame realization $q\simeq0.17729809$,
the required bright fraction is about $0.30388$, not just $1/4$.
As $t\to\infty$, the remaining score fraction is $1-p$. These are
amplitude-score statements; a third C5 block has not been identified
with $d$, and three residual norms are not three scalar probe modes.

\subsection{Relative frequency detuning can produce slow decay}

After subtracting a common frequency, two different probe frequencies
give, in the bright/dark basis,
\[
 H=\begin{pmatrix}0&\delta\\\delta&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \delta=(\epsilon_1-\epsilon_2)/2,\qquad
 A=-iH-\tfrac12\Gamma
   =\begin{pmatrix}-\lambda/2&-i\delta\\-i\delta&0\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Here $\delta$ is real; a common frequency only rotates the entire
state. The exact amplitude eigenvalues are
\[
 s_\pm=-\lambda/4\pm\sqrt{\lambda^2/16-\delta^2}.
\]
When $0<|\delta|\ll\lambda$, the slow eigenmode has
\[
 s_+=-2\delta^2/\lambda+O(\delta^4/\lambda^3),
 \qquad \text{asymptotic score-decay rate}
       =4\delta^2/\lambda+O(\delta^4/\lambda^3).
\]
An initially dark state is not exactly this eigenmode, but its
late-time decay is controlled by it. Small detuning therefore
removes exact darkness while leaving a parametrically slow tail.
Passive rephasing does not create this effect: it conjugates both
Hamiltonian and vertices and leaves the decay spectrum unchanged.
A physical accumulated relative phase must arise from an actual
frequency/coupling change, not just a change of notation.

\paragraph{HC compatibility is a separate test.}
If the fixed native involution really exchanges these same two
probes, $J=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1)$ in the bright/dark basis and
$C=(I+J)/2$ excludes $d$. Then
\[
 [H,J]=\begin{pmatrix}0&-2\delta\\2\delta&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad \|[H,J]\|_F=2\sqrt2|\delta|.
\]
The detuned model is not HC-preserving on the fixed even sector.
It cannot be used to justify an admissible native update by simply
claiming independent frame shifts. Either the readout really uses
only the bright even sector with a dynamics preserving it, or the
source must license a different embedding, an internal even-sector
slow mode, or a consistently transported selector. The active
source does not identify ART/QFT probes with global matter/tachyon
frames, so that exchange itself remains conditional.

\subsection{A finite-time test stronger than instantaneous rank}

For a constant finite-dimensional outgoing reduction
$A=-iH-\Gamma/2$ with $H=H^\dagger$ and $\Gamma\geq0$, define
\[
 W_\tau=\int_0^\tau e^{A^\dagger s}\Gamma e^{As}\,ds.
\]
Differentiating $e^{A^\dagger s}e^{As}$ gives exactly
\[
 e^{A^\dagger\tau}e^{A\tau}=I-W_\tau.
\]
For an isometric allowed-state inclusion $E$, uniform squared-norm
contraction by $3/4$ is therefore equivalent to
\[
 E^\dagger W_\tau E\geq\tfrac14 I.
\]
Moreover $W_\tau$ has a zero direction precisely when a nonzero
allowed $r$ satisfies $\Gamma^{1/2}e^{As}r=0$ for every
$s\in[0,\tau]$. This identifies the invariant dark subspace,
not just an instantaneous rank deficiency. A positive minimum
eigenvalue can still be too small for the specified threshold.
For a different norm-whitened residual extraction $L_5$, the actual
inequality is instead
\[
 E^\dagger e^{A^\dagger\tau}L_5^\dagger L_5 e^{A\tau}E
 \leq\tfrac34 E^\dagger L_5^\dagger L_5 E.
\]
Nonlinear residuals require their evaluated update, not substitution
of this linear extraction without proof. Memory kernels require a
corresponding enlarged evolution or a controlled approximation.

\subsection{One explicit finite-band density, not a native assignment}

To exhibit what a spectral calculation would actually require,
consider the envelope operator $H_X=-iv\partial_x$ on
$L^2(\mathbb R)$, $v>0$, and coupling profile
$f=L^{-1/2}\mathbf1_{[0,L]}$ with strength $g$.
Using the unitary Fourier convention, direct transformation gives
\[
 \mathcal J_X(\nu)=\frac{|g|^2L}{2\pi v}
   \operatorname{sinc}^2\!\left(\frac{\nu L}{2v}\right),
 \qquad \operatorname{sinc}(x)=\sin(x)/x,
\]
and translation overlap gives the exact memory kernel
\[
 K(t)=|g|^2\langle f,e^{-iH_Xt}f\rangle
     =|g|^2(1-v|t|/L)_+.
\]
At the envelope resonance $\nu=0$, $2\pi\mathcal J_X(0)
=|g|^2L/v=\lambda$, while
$\int_0^\infty K(t)dt=\lambda/2$. This reproduces the Markov
amplitude coefficient only when replacing the finite-memory
convolution by its area is justified; it is not an exact exponential
law from $t=0$. With empty bath the exact visible derivative at
$t=0$ is zero. The bath translation is one-way, but a finite spatial
coupling region still has a finite memory time $L/v$.

This is an envelope/collective-mode example: $H_X$ has spectrum
$\mathbb R$, not a bounded-below physical energy spectrum, and a
single amplitude coupled over a finite region is not a proof of a
local relativistic field theory. Neither $g$, $L$, the polarization
rank nor this dispersion has been extracted from $\Omega_\perp$.
A flat free test field also does not automatically solve the
source's connection equation with nonzero total current.

\paragraph{Verification and native conclusion.}
The executable check now independently integrates $W_\tau$ and
verifies the finite-time flux identity, exact dark-state preservation,
slow detuning-dependent decay and the nonzero fixed-$J$ commutator.
All eighteen tests pass. The source supplies a candidate channel
architecture, not the numerical spectral and readout dictionary.
The concrete native next step is to evaluate its open polarization
matrix and effective Hamiltonian, determine which dark/slow modes
the C5 readout sees, and test the finite-time inequality together
with the actual Q/HC/C4/C6 guards. Slow decay is now explained by a
specific conditional mechanism, not identified as a native fact.

\section{An HC-even slow supply mode already allowed by the triadic block}
\label{sec:hc-even-slow-supply}

The preceding odd dark mode need not be imported into the HC-even
sector. Under the explicit probe-exchange embedding used in
Section~\ref{sec:native-normalized-readout}, the source's existing
triad has an even probe $a=(\Psi_1+\Psi_2)/\sqrt2$, an odd probe
$d=(\Psi_1-\Psi_2)/\sqrt2$, and an even intermediate component
$b=\Psi_\perp$. Hence
\[
 J_s=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1,1),\qquad
 C_s=\operatorname{diag}(1,0,1)
\]
in the $(a,d,b)$ basis. HC-even does not mean one-dimensional.
This embedding is a conditional realization of the source, not
an established identification of global frame labels.

\subsection{Source-shaped conservative coupling inside the even sector}

With the source symmetry conditions
$\Omega_1=\Omega_2$, $G_{12}=G_{21}$, $V_1=V_2$ and $U_1=U_2$,
the even resonance block is already
\[
 \Omega_+=\begin{pmatrix}
  \Omega_1+g_Y\mathcal T_{12}&\sqrt2\chi\mathcal T_{1\perp}\\
  \sqrt2\chi\mathcal T_{\perp1}&\Omega_\perp
 \end{pmatrix}.
\]
Assume a common positive-current time reduction turns this into
the Hermitian scalar-mode block
\[
 H_+=\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon_a&k\\k^*&\epsilon_b\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Only in a reduction with the requisite common kinetic normalization
can $k$ be read directly as $\sqrt2\chi\mathcal T_{1\perp}$.
The general operator-valued vertices need not be scalar or mutually
commuting. For this scalar block a fixed internal basis can make
$k\geq0$ real. Subtract the common frequency $\epsilon_a$ and put
$\Delta=\epsilon_b-\epsilon_a$.

An outgoing Markov load on the intermediate component gives
\[
 \frac{d}{dt}\begin{pmatrix}a\\b\end{pmatrix}
 =A_+\begin{pmatrix}a\\b\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 A_+=\begin{pmatrix}0&-ik\\-ik&-\gamma/2-i\Delta\end{pmatrix},
 \quad \gamma>0.
\]
The load is the effective result of eliminating outgoing spatial
modes, not an imaginary mass inserted into the native conservative
resonance. Its spectral density and no-return conditions still
need the preceding native audit. It is not a new independent
physical component added by fiat to the finite triad.

Extended by an arbitrary uncoupled odd probe, both the Hermitian
coupling and the damping matrix commute with $J_s$ and $C_s$.
Thus $d(0)=0$ implies $d(t)=0$ exactly, even for $k\ne0$ and
$\Delta\ne0$. The bath can also be assigned the same even parity
in a conservative dilation. For the full field this requires
kinetic, connection and domain intertwining as well; a potential
commutator alone does not suffice. All participating modes must
lie inside a licensed reducing $Q$ sector. This calculation does
not evaluate the native C4/C6 guard functions.

\subsection{Exact poles and the slow-detuning law}

Writing $D=\gamma/2+i\Delta$, the exact amplitude poles obey
\[
 s^2+Ds+k^2=0,\qquad
 s_\pm=-D/2\pm\sqrt{D^2/4-k^2}.
\]
For $k\ll|D|$, the pole continuous from $s=0$ at $k=0$ is
\[
 s_{\rm slow}=-\frac{k^2}{D}+O(k^4/|D|^3),\qquad
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 -2\operatorname{Re}s_{\rm slow}
  =\frac{\gamma k^2}{\Delta^2+\gamma^2/4}
      +O(k^4/|D|^3).}
\]
The boxed quantity is the late-time squared-amplitude decay rate
of the slow eigenmode, not an exact finite-time score law for an
arbitrary initial state. At fixed $k,\gamma$, frequency mismatch
suppresses the drain through the intermediate component. Large
$\gamma$ can also suppress transfer at resonance in the regime
$k\ll\gamma$, where the rate is $4k^2/\gamma$.
This is distinct from the previous forbidden even--odd detuning:
both components here are even under the fixed selector.

For prepared $a(0)=1$, $b(0)=0$, the exact transfer matrix is
\[
 T(t)=e^{-Dt/2}\left[
 \cosh(\zeta t)I+
 \frac{\sinh(\zeta t)}{\zeta}(A_++D I/2)\right],
 \qquad \zeta=\sqrt{D^2/4-k^2},
\]
with the continuous quotient $t$ at $\zeta=0$. The illustrative
model units $\gamma=1$, $k=0.1$, $t=20$ give
\[
 |a(20)|^2\simeq
 \begin{cases}
 0.4743633505,&\Delta=0,\\
 0.9503816423,&\Delta=2.
 \end{cases}
\]
The exact slow eigenmode score-decay rates are respectively
$0.04174243050$ and $0.002337735282$. These numbers are calculated
from declared model parameters, not measured or inferred native
frame values. A frequency mismatch corresponds to an accumulated
relative phase, but it is a probe--intermediate mismatch. Calling
it a matter--tachyon mismatch requires the missing branch embedding.

\paragraph{The MM degeneracy constraint.}
Under the earlier strong premise $\mathcal M C=\lambda_0 C$, if
both retained modes lie in the same licensed even intersection,
the compressed MM contribution is $\lambda_0 I$. It cannot alone
generate their frequency difference or off-diagonal mixing.
A common scalar $m_0 I$ likewise cancels from $\Delta$ in the
common time normalization. The already displayed triadic and
connection contributions must supply any nontrivial difference
or coupling, or the stronger degeneracy premise must not apply
to that realization. Arbitrary independent frame phases are not
a substitute for evaluating these source terms.

\subsection{Conservation does not imply visible-only contraction}

The reduced block obeys the exact flux identity
\[
 \frac{d}{dt}(|a|^2+|b|^2)=-\gamma|b|^2.
\]
Adding the outgoing history integral restores unit total norm
for the prepared unit state. But
\[
 \frac{d}{dt}|a|^2=2k\operatorname{Im}(a^*b)
\]
has no fixed sign. With $\gamma=\Delta=0$, the prepared solution
is $a=\cos(kt)$, $b=-i\sin(kt)$: its visible score vanishes at
$kt=\pi/2$ and returns to one at $kt=\pi$. HC remains preserved
throughout. Outgoing propagation must control this internal
feeding memory as well as distant reflected waves.

There is a stronger quantifier obstruction. If the readout were
only $L_5(a,b)=a$ and arbitrary hidden $b$ were admissible, then
\[
 |L_5 T(\tau)r|^2\leq\tfrac34|L_5 r|^2
 \quad\text{for all }r
\]
would require $T_{ab}(\tau)=0$, by testing $r=(0,b)$.
For generic $k\ne0$ this entry is nonzero. In the example
$\Delta=2$, $|T_{ab}(20)|^2\simeq0.002227115239$:
an initially invisible state produces a positive visible score
and violates any such uniform multiplicative bound. Empty hidden
initial data licenses the narrower condition
$|T_{aa}(\tau)|^2\leq3/4$ for that preparation; it is not
automatically renewed at each step because $b$ is subsequently
populated. A history/preparation constraint, evaluated incoming
bound, or enlarged native residual is needed. Actual C5 is not
yet identified with this scalar extraction.

\paragraph{Verification and result.}
Twenty-one executable tests now pass. The new tests verify zero
HC commutators and odd leakage, exact two-mode return without
the load, detuning-suppressed visible decay, and nonzero hidden
supply. Independent integration checks retained plus emitted
norm to about $1.6\times10^{-36}$ in the declared example.
The existing source architecture permits an HC-even slow-mode
mechanism without the earlier even--odd violation. It does not
yet identify that mechanism with the failed third C5 bound.
Its decisive native obligations are evaluated $k,\Delta,\gamma$,
an admissible initial/history domain, and the actual readout and
guard inequalities; a scalar spectral MM term cannot provide
the missing detuning by itself.

\section{Repeated visible lowering without resetting the intermediate mode}
\label{sec:invariant-supply-cone}

The hidden-supply obstruction can be resolved on a specified
invariant preparation domain, rather than by changing C5's score
or deleting hidden amplitude at each step. This section proves
that resolution for the preceding constant outgoing reduction.
It does not assert that the native witness domain or physical
sampling clock has already been identified with it.

\subsection{The invariant graph and exact transient coordinates}

Let $k>0$, $\gamma>0$, $D=\gamma/2+i\Delta$, and suppose the
two poles of $A_+$ are distinct. Label them $s_s,s_f$ with
$\operatorname{Re}s_s\geq\operatorname{Re}s_f$ and choose the
slow pole continuous from zero in the weak-coupling regime.
Both real parts are strictly negative: an undamped eigenvector
would have $b=0$ by the flux identity, and the second field equation
would then force $a=0$. This argument uses a positive current metric.

Set
\[
 z=\frac{i s_s}{k},\qquad \eta=b-za.
\]
The characteristic equation implies
$-ik-Dz=s_sz$. Substitution into the field equations therefore
gives exactly
\[
 \dot a=s_s a-ik\eta,\qquad
 \dot\eta=s_f\eta.
\]
The graph $b=za$ is invariant, lies entirely in the even sector,
and has $a(t)=e^{s_st}a(0)$. Its hidden amplitude is retained and
decays together with the visible amplitude; it is not reset.
For a sampling interval $\tau>0$, put
\[
 u=e^{s_s\tau},\qquad v=e^{s_f\tau},\qquad
 F=-ik\frac{u-v}{s_s-s_f}.
\]
The full sampled dynamics, including preparation error, is
\[
 a_{n+1}=u a_n+F\eta_n,\qquad
 \eta_{n+1}=v\eta_n.
\]
This is an exact change of coordinates in the model, not a
Markov approximation additional to the already assumed outgoing
load. At coincident poles a different Jordan-limit estimate is
needed; the executable certificate rejects that special case.

\subsection{A robust invariant cone and a repeated bound}

For $\varepsilon>0$ define the nonzero preparation cone
\[
 \mathcal K_\varepsilon=
 \{(a,b):a\ne0,\ |b-za|\leq\varepsilon|a|\}.
\]
It excludes arbitrary initially invisible hidden states, which
caused the earlier uniform-bound obstruction. On this cone,
\[
 \ell |a_n|\leq|a_{n+1}|\leq m|a_n|,
 \qquad
 \ell=|u|-|F|\varepsilon,\quad
 m=|u|+|F|\varepsilon.
\]
If
\[
 \boxed{\quad \ell>0,\qquad |v|\leq\ell,
              \qquad m\leq\sqrt{3/4},\quad}
\]
then the cone is invariant and every sampled visible score obeys
\[
 0<\ell^2|a_n|^2\leq|a_{n+1}|^2
       \leq\tfrac34|a_n|^2.
\]
Indeed $|\eta_{n+1}|\leq|v|\varepsilon|a_n|
\leq\varepsilon|a_{n+1}|$. Induction proves the inequality for
every finite step with the same update, without a hidden reset.
The lower bound excludes exact visible zero at finite steps.
It does not license native limit closure or removal of C4/C6.

For distinct poles with
$\operatorname{Re}s_f<\operatorname{Re}s_s$, the ratio
$|v|/|u|$ tends to zero as $\tau$ grows, and
$|F|/|u|$ tends to $k/|s_s-s_f|$.
Thus choosing $\varepsilon<|s_s-s_f|/k$ and then a sufficiently
large $\tau$ makes the boxed conditions possible. Degenerate
real parts do not automatically yield this cone argument.
The required sampling interval may be long; a freely chosen
interval is not a prediction of a native QAM step duration.

\paragraph{Explicit example, including empty initial intermediate field.}
With the previous declared model values
$\gamma=1$, $k=0.1$, $\Delta=2$, choose
\[
 \tau=\frac{\log(1/2)}{2\operatorname{Re}s_s}
        \simeq296.50370843,\qquad \varepsilon=1.
\]
The exact graph coefficient is
$z\simeq-0.04697433276-0.01168867641i$. The cone conditions give
\[
 0.4528600456\,|a_n|^2
 \leq |a_{n+1}|^2
 \leq0.5494734940\,|a_n|^2 <\tfrac34|a_n|^2.
\]
Since $|z|<1$, $b_0=0$ belongs to this cone as well:
$\eta_0=-za_0$. Subsequent $b_n$ is evolved, not emptied. The
test follows twenty consecutive updates from this preparation
and from cone-boundary phases; the largest empty-preparation
step ratio is $1/2$. The algebraic proof covers all cone phases
and all finite steps, not just the tested samples. This longer
sampling interval resolves the example's failure at $t=20$;
it does not show that the same threshold held at that earlier
time or at every continuous-time instant.

\subsection{Explicit transfer and residual-dictionary error budgets}

Let an evaluated one-step deviation contribute $\delta a,\delta b$
with
\[
 |\delta a|,|\delta b|\leq d S|a_n|,\qquad
 S=1+|z|+\varepsilon.
\]
For example, an entrywise bound $d$ on a linear transfer-matrix
error gives this inequality since $|b_n|\leq(|z|+\varepsilon)|a_n|$.
Independent incoming forcing must be included in the same budget;
it is not controlled by a transfer-matrix comparison alone.
The perturbed visible upper/lower factors become
$m+dS$ and $\ell-dS$, while
\[
 |\eta_{n+1}|\leq
 \bigl(|v|\varepsilon+d(1+|z|)S\bigr)|a_n|.
\]
Hence both cone invariance and the $3/4$ score bound survive if
\[
 d\leq\min\left\{
  \frac{\sqrt{3/4}-m}{S},\quad
  \frac{\varepsilon(\ell-|v|)}{S^2}
 \right\}.
\]
In the example this separate entrywise budget is approximately
$0.06090617040$. An arbitrary matrix error is not thereby proved
conservative or Q/HC/guard-preserving: those are independent
requirements on the evaluated dynamics. Native finite-band,
nonlinear, gravitational and return effects have not been shown
to lie within this budget. It specifies what they must satisfy.

The frozen r04 source defines its score as the sum of the squared
norms of three fixed residual blocks. It does not authorize a new
Lyapunov metric or counting the intermediate/history norm instead.
To transfer the present visible bound, suppose an actual witness
trajectory has been evaluated and satisfies, in that same fixed
normalized residual space,
\[
 R_5(W_n)=a_n r_*+e_n,\qquad r_*\ne0,\qquad
 \|e_n\|\leq\delta_R\,|a_n|\|r_*\|,\quad 0\leq\delta_R<1.
\]
This is a dictionary test, not a definition of the native residuals.
If the unperturbed visible upper factor is $m$, then
\[
 \frac{\Phi_5(W_{n+1})}{\Phi_5(W_n)}
 \leq m^2\left(\frac{1+\delta_R}{1-\delta_R}\right)^2
 \leq\frac34
\]
provided
\[
 \delta_R\leq
 \frac{\sqrt{3/4}-m}{\sqrt{3/4}+m}.
\]
For the example this separate dictionary tolerance is about
$0.07762170799$. Transfer and dictionary budgets cannot each be
spent to their maximum simultaneously: with both present use
$m+dS$ instead of $m$ in the last condition. These tolerances
neither manufacture residual values nor prove the actual guards
$\Lambda_5^{\rm new}=A_4^{\rm abs}=U_6^{\rm all}=0$.

\paragraph{Verification and remaining identification.}
Twenty-seven executable tests pass. The added checks independently
compare the graph with a matrix exponential, test exact upper/lower
extremizing phases and twenty-step cone evolution, verify empty
hidden preparation without resets, reject excessive tolerance and
the coincident-pole case, and check perturbed transfer matrices
within the stated separate error budget. The helper accepts
\texttt{--cone-data} with declared load, coupling, detuning,
eigenmode score and cone tolerance; it never marks a native
identification as certified on the basis of these numbers alone.

The repeated-lowering obstruction is now solved for the specified
outgoing model and preparation domain, with an explicit robustness
margin and unchanged visible-score meaning. For the native paper,
the unresolved step is to produce a guard-admissible witness map,
evaluate its fixed three-block dictionary and physical time scale,
and bound its actual dynamics by this certificate. Unevaluated
native residual symbols cannot establish that step. No C4
absorption, C6 unblocking, exact finite zero or physical wormhole
claim is implied.

\section{A source audit and fixed three-block C5 dictionary test}
\label{sec:fixed-native-c5-dictionary-audit}

This step investigates the existing residual definitions rather
than assigning a different score to the outgoing model. The
three r04 block names describe bounded, exterior and mixed fit
residuals. They are not three scalar amplitudes and are not an
identification of matter, tachyon and photon frames.

\subsection{What the frozen source actually provides}

In the frozen v4.7.0 source, \texttt{def:v470-r01-admissibility-residual}
introduces five residual slots. The extracted C5 candidate in
\texttt{def:v470-r02-extracted-candidate-family} is the bounded
finite witness-fit interface, not the full intermediate field.
Its symbolic r02 row in
\texttt{def:v470-r02-symbolic-residual-ledger} is
\[
 C_5:\quad
 (\rho_{\rm bd},\rho_{\rm ext},\rho_{\rm mix},
   \rho_{\rm leak},\rho_{\rm fit})
 =(0,0,\epsilon^{\rm fit}_{\rm mix},0,\rho_{\rm fit}).
\]
The source explicitly calls these named slots non-numerical.
The r04 definition
\texttt{def:v470-r04-c5-lowering-score} later assigns to fit
packages $W$ the three-block score
\[
 \Phi_5(W)=\|\rho_{5,\rm bd}(W)\|^2
          +\|\rho_{5,\rm ext}(W)\|^2
          +\|\rho_{5,\rm mix}(W)\|^2.
\]
It gives ledger values, but not evaluation formulas, norm-space
dimensions or a map from the five candidate slots to those three
witness residuals. If an independent bridge identified the r04
components with the r02 C5 row without alteration, the bounded
and exterior components would vanish and only the mixed fit
component would contribute. The superscripted r04 witness
$W_5^{\rm bd}$ does not itself mean that only the bounded residual
component is nonzero. That bridge cannot be inferred from its name.

The independent slots $\rho_{\rm leak}$ and $\rho_{\rm fit}$
must not silently be appended to r04's score or absorbed into it.
Likewise C4's persistent mixed leakage is explicitly excluded
from the r04 fit-lowering test. Equality of names containing
``mixed'' does not identify the C5 fit rest with protected C4.
The active older MM/HC field equations do not supply the missing
numeric dictionary or identify these residuals with wave amplitudes.

\paragraph{The concrete lens readout is not yet that bridge.}
The source's bounded symmetric bifocal refit does define
\[
 \Delta_{\rm lens}(x;A,c)
  =\left|\|x+ce\|+\|x-ce\|-2A\right|.
\]
For fixed samples, axes and fit parameters this residual is
unchanged by decay of a separate field amplitude. To obtain
an update one needs an evaluated map from the field/witness
state to $(x,A,c,e)$ and a typed assignment of those sample
residuals to the r04 blocks. The earlier synthetic lens inverse
provides neither the original scan nor that native assignment.
Similarly the equation defect of an exact field solution is
already zero; it is not automatically a positive C5 fit residual.

\subsection{Deriving a dictionary response from evaluated witness data}

Suppose a fit package $W$ and a fixed normalized residual
dictionary have actually been evaluated. A proposed local witness
chart $W=W_*+E_a a+E_b b$ gives, for differentiable residuals,
\[
 \rho_{5,j}(W)=c_j+L_j a+B_j b+\mathcal N_j(a,b),
 \quad c_j=\rho_{5,j}(W_*),\quad
 L_j=D\rho_{5,j}(W_*)E_a,\quad
 B_j=D\rho_{5,j}(W_*)E_b,
\]
where $j\in\{\rm bd,ext,mix\}$ and $\mathcal N_j$ is the
Taylor remainder. A general real-linear response needs both
complex quadratures (or conjugate columns); the scalar complex-
linear formula here is a restricted case, not a universal model
of nonlinear tensor residuals. If the derivative is Lipschitz
with constant $M_j$, the local remainder is bounded by
$M_j\|E_a a+E_b b\|^2/2$ in its declared chart. Chart existence,
norms, bounds and preservation of the actual guards need proof.

For an exact affine dictionary, stack the three fixed block
vectors as $c,L,B$. In the preceding supply coordinates
$b=za+\eta$, put $h=L+zB$. Then exactly
\[
 R_5=c+ha+B\eta.
\]
On the invariant graph $\eta=0$, if $c=0$ and $h\ne0$,
\[
 R_5(W_{n+1})=uR_5(W_n),\qquad
 \Phi_5(W_{n+1})=|u|^2\Phi_5(W_n).
\]
Thus the readout need not ignore the intermediate component:
\emph{any fixed homogeneous complex-linear three-block dictionary}
has this identity on the graph, provided it does not annihilate
that graph. Every nonzero block scales with the same factor.
The conclusion is derived from the restricted state evolution,
not from setting all three native residuals equal to a guessed
amplitude. If $h=0$, the graph is invisible and its zero score
cannot certify strict positive lowering.

On the preparation cone $|\eta|\leq\varepsilon|a|$, let
$H=\|h\|$ and $B_0=\|B\|$. With $c=0$ and
$H>\varepsilon B_0$,
\[
 (H-\varepsilon B_0)|a|\leq\|R_5\|
       \leq(H+\varepsilon B_0)|a|.
\]
The earlier state bounds $\ell|a_n|\leq|a_{n+1}|\leq m|a_n|$
therefore imply
\[
 \ell^2\left(\frac{H-\varepsilon B_0}{H+\varepsilon B_0}\right)^2
 \leq\frac{\Phi_5(W_{n+1})}{\Phi_5(W_n)}
 \leq m^2\left(\frac{H+\varepsilon B_0}{H-\varepsilon B_0}\right)^2.
\]
This is a fixed-dictionary contraction and positivity test.
Separate native guard tests remain indispensable.

\subsection{A persistent offset prevents uniform lowering, not every finite fit}

If $c\ne0$ and the field state tends to zero, continuity gives
$R_5\to c$ and $\Phi_5\to\|c\|^2>0$. Hence a uniform
$\Phi_{n+1}\leq3\Phi_n/4$ for every step is impossible.
More strongly, a homogeneous linear state update cannot satisfy
that uniform inequality on a whole preparation cone containing
arbitrarily small state amplitudes: take the amplitude to zero
at one step and both readouts tend to $c$.

This does not require C5 to be completely solved before a finite
lowering test. For $0<\ell<1$, $|a_0|=A_0>0$ and steps
$0\leq n\leq N$, the lower bound gives $|a_n|\geq\ell^N A_0$.
If $\mathcal N_n$ denotes the evaluated stacked nonlinear
remainder and
\[
 \delta_N:=\frac{\varepsilon B_0}{H}
       +\frac{\|c\|}{H\ell^N A_0}
       +\sup_{0\leq n\leq N}
          \frac{\|\mathcal N_n\|}{H|a_n|}<1,
\]
then for all $N$ steps
\[
 \frac{\Phi_5(W_{n+1})}{\Phi_5(W_n)}
 \leq m^2\left(\frac{1+\delta_N}{1-\delta_N}\right)^2.
\]
Requiring this upper bound to be at most $3/4$ proves finite
lowering without setting $c=0$. The lower bound on residual
norm also keeps all tested finite scores positive. As $N$ grows,
the fixed offset consumes the margin; it cannot be discarded
or identified with an exactly closed endpoint.
If $W_*$ itself is a proposed zero-residual witness, its existence
and admissibility cannot be assumed merely to obtain $c=0$.
In particular, the frozen finite r04 ledger contains no admissible
zero-score row: its two zero-score rows are blocked by C4 or C6.
A finite estimate with nonzero $c$ avoids assuming such a witness.

For nonlinear residuals there is another issue: their degree of
homogeneity must be determined. On an invariant graph, a norm-
homogeneous block of degree $p_j$ scales in squared norm by
$|u|^{2p_j}$. Bilinear current defects may have degree two,
whereas a frozen structural fit can have degree zero. Their
scaling is not automatically the field-amplitude scaling, and
the field equation alone does not decide those degrees.

\subsection{Executable audit with the dictionary held fixed}

The helper's \texttt{--readout-data} accepts exactly the named
\texttt{bd}, \texttt{ext}, \texttt{mix} blocks in their supplied
norm-whitened coordinates. Each affine block has fixed
\texttt{offset}, \texttt{probe} and \texttt{hidden} vectors;
the evaluated two-mode transfer, graph coefficient and cone
tolerance are separate inputs. Nonempty block dimensions may
differ. Explicit real/imaginary complex entries are supported.
No fitted rotation, block reweighting, leakage slot or history
norm is inserted into the score. Its fields are numerical
diagnostics, not interval-certified native theorems.

For a general supplied matrix $T$, the audit also tests graph
defect rather than assuming an invariant eigenvector:
\[
 u=T_{aa}+zT_{ab},\quad F=T_{ab},\quad
 r=T_{ba}+zT_{bb}-zu,\quad v=T_{bb}-zF,
\]
so that $a'=ua+F\eta$ and $\eta'=ra+v\eta$.
The sufficient cone condition becomes
$|r|+|v|\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon\ell$ with
$\ell=|u|-|F|\varepsilon>0$.
Native provenance, nonlinear dictionary validity, clock
identification and the evaluated guard functions are never
inferred from passing this numeric test.

In explicitly synthetic examples with four residual coordinates
distributed across the three blocks, the exact graph gives
ratio $1/2$ in each block; a cone of tolerance $0.1$ gives
an upper total-score bound of about $0.53927056$.
A fixed mixed-block offset of one instead gives asymptotic score
one and a twentieth step ratio of about $0.99262890$.
An offset of $10^{-6}$ is still nonzero but passes the finite
twenty-step bound. These are counterexamples and construction
checks, not recovered native C5 measurements.

\paragraph{Verification and result.}
Thirty-four tests pass, including fixed three-block propagation,
different block dimensions, constant-offset obstruction, finite
lowering with a small nonzero offset, an invisible graph,
failed cone invariance, malformed inputs and explicit complex
encoding. The source audit fixes the roles of the named slots
and proves a transfer criterion for an independently evaluated
dictionary. It does not recover numeric r04 residuals from the
ledger: that evaluation map remains unprovided in the examined
source. A concrete native continuation must supply the fit
package and its typed block functions, or the original calibration
data and their block assignment, then apply these tests with
the actual guards. No complete prior solution of C5 is required
for the finite test, but the missing dictionary cannot be
replaced by synthetic vectors.

\section{Working-source corrections to the MM/HC and OP1 premises}
\label{sec:mm-hc-exponent-source-corrections}

The canonical modular working sources have been corrected at
\texttt{lem:diagonal-traversal-scalar-action},
\texttt{thm:hc-spectral-projector},
\texttt{thm:self-consistent-gL}, \texttt{prop:exponent-5} and
\texttt{rem:exact-sc}. Historical labels are retained so existing
references remain resolvable; a historical label is not the current
theorematic status. The frozen public v4.7.0 source and release
artifacts are unchanged. The same issues remain in that archived
version and require a later corrected release, not silent replacement.

\subsection{The spectral identification is explicitly conditional}

In a bounded complex Hilbert realization, commutation with an
orthogonal $C$ preserves its image, but does not make that image
a single eigenspace. The corrected scalar lemma requires a complete
eigenvector decomposition on the image with one eigenvalue.
The old cascade-to-Jordan argument did not supply this condition,
and the cited closure-forcing lemma did not establish irreducibility
of the required representation. No such derivation is now assumed.

Even $\mathcal MC=C\mathcal M=\lambda_0 C$ does not force $C$
to be the full spectral projector. The corrected theorem additionally
requires
\[
 \ker(\mathcal M-\lambda_0 I)\cap\ker C=\{0\},
\]
within an explicitly assumed orthogonal pure-point decomposition.
Then an eigenvector $x$ satisfies
$(I-C)x\in\ker(\mathcal M-\lambda_0 I)\cap\ker C$, hence
$x=Cx$, proving full eigenspace equality. The examples
$\mathcal M=\mathrm{diag}(1,2,3)$, $C=\mathrm{diag}(1,1,0)$ and
$\mathcal M=\mathrm{diag}(2,2,3)$, $C=\mathrm{diag}(1,0,0)$
separately exhibit the missing single-eigenvalue and complement
conditions. A projection also does not determine the operator's
action on its complement.

Immediate singlet, frame-readout and meta-field uses now inherit
the spectral hypotheses; color representation alone does not
identify individual $\mathcal M$ eigendirections. Native closure
classification, kinetic compatibility and physical interpretation
are separate obligations. These corrections do not establish the
missing premise in an actual MM/HC realization, nor repair every
historical closure argument. In particular they do not justify
promoting projected fixed-point data to equality of the original
unprojected state without its own proof.

\subsection{An inverse exponent cannot predict its own inputs}

For $g>1$ and $0<\beta<1/\sqrt3$, define
\[
 A(g,\beta)=\frac{\gamma_1}{4\pi|\log(\beta\sqrt3)|},\qquad
 n_{\rm exact}(g,\beta)=\frac{\log A(g,\beta)}{\log g}.
\]
Then $g^{n_{\rm exact}}=A$ is an identity for every allowed pair.
The additional relation $\beta(g)=\sqrt{(g-1)/(g+1)}$ leaves it
an identity for all $1<g<2$. It cannot determine $g$ from the
first zero ordinate alone. The inverse diagnostic gives about
$5.00584$ for the independent rounded pair $(1.864,0.5493)$,
but about $5.00293$ if the frame relation is enforced exactly
at $g=1.864$. These are distinct input choices, not a structural
derivation of exponent five.

With the exponent independently fixed to $5$ as an ansatz, a genuine
scalar equation remains:
\[
 f(g)=\tfrac12\log3+\tfrac12\log\frac{g-1}{g+1}
              +\frac{a}{g^5}=0,\qquad a=\gamma_1/(4\pi).
\]
For $0<a<27/20$, $f'>0$ follows from
$g^6/(g^2-1)\geq27/4>5a$. Together with
$f(1+)=-\infty$ and $f(2)=a/32>0$ this proves a unique root
in $(1,2)$. At the stated first-zero input it is
$g\simeq1.863662249889$, $\beta\simeq0.549175395418$.
The approximately $0.03957\%$ difference from the inherited
comparison value $1.8644$ is retained as a numerical comparison,
not as a first-principles solution of OP1 or a physical uncertainty.
The exponent and the correction's lattice origin remain open.

\paragraph{Verification.}
Ten added standard-library regression tests check exact rational
matrix counterexamples, a positive noncoordinate projector example,
the inverse-exponent identity and domain, rounded-input sensitivity,
the derivative bound and the conditional model root. Together with
the existing framework tests and six reading-route checks, all 63
audit tests pass. The separate
34 C5 relay/readout tests remain model diagnostics, not a substitute
for the corrected source premises or a native closure proof.

\paragraph{Editorial reading-route pilot.}
The modular main source now begins with a compact QAM/MML/MMA
interface. Its five original formal-language modules are retained
unchanged and included exactly once in a technical appendix.
The MM/HC and fixed-exponent statements keep their hypotheses,
conclusions and proof ideas in the main text; complete proofs,
counterexamples, input conventions and the verification ledger
are directly linked in the new core-proof appendix. No anonymous
token is substituted for a missing argument.

The OP5 balance has an explicit reading contract: endpoint
regularization, full-sum control and a remainder estimate remain
obligations, and the separate C5 fit interface is not silently
identified with that balance. Existing quantitative blocks are
retained for audit rather than hidden or certified by this editorial
pass. The routing tests check unique inclusion, retained labels,
visible hypotheses and the new reference targets, not mathematical
truth. Published artifacts and historical sources remain unchanged;
no v4.8.0 release is declared by this working-source reorganization.

Andre Massing, NTNU, \emph{Weak formulation and well-posedness of the
advection-reaction-diffusion problem},
\href{https://www.math.ntnu.no/emner/MA8502/2024h/lectures/html/chapter_03/weak_form_adr.html}{lecture notes}.
The multichannel time-dependent energy identity and the source and sampling
estimates above are derived explicitly in this note.

\paragraph{Fluid references and verification.}
NASA Glenn Research Center,
\href{https://www1.grc.nasa.gov/beginners-guide-to-aeronautics/navier-strokes-equation/}{Navier--Stokes equations}
and
\href{https://www.grc.nasa.gov/WWW/K-12/airplane/reynolds.html}{Reynolds number}.
The divergence, advection, equation residual and mean squared velocity for
the displayed shear field were independently checked by symbolic
differentiation and integration. The resulting divergence, advection and
equation residual are identically zero. This is an analytic solution check,
not a numerical simulation or a validation of the proposed native fluid map.

Ko Honda, \emph{Differential Geometry}, vector bundles, local trivializations
and sections,
\href{https://www.math.ucla.edu/~honda/math535a/notes.pdf}{author's lecture notes}.
The contraction construction and derivative counterexample above are
explicit calculations for this working note.

H. Jerome Keisler, \emph{Foundations of Infinitesimal Calculus},
Sections 1B, 3C, 9A and 15C,

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Native continuation.} Specify the MM/HC witness domain and
component residual functions; construct its witness update; prove residual
intertwining, domain and guard invariance; then prove native closure-class
preservation before using QAM admissible transport. A native limit requires
its own state topology and admissible limit witness. The scalar or model
limit alone does not establish global all-readout closure.
